3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.88545pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.89238pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.84525pt\raise-37.42137pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.08252pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.8941pt\raise-28.33331pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ }$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.89238pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.89238pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 67.89238pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

We call the
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-monomorphism
e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}=\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}
the
canonical inclusion
of the
prolongation in the original PDE.

(b) If the canonical inclusion is in fact an isomorphism,

e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{E}
, we say that the
generalized PDE
\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
is

formally integrable
.

(c) If
e^{\prime}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y^{\prime}
is
another generalized PDE, then an
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-morphism

\phi\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is said to
preserve
formal solutions
if for any parametrized family of formal solutions

\sigma^{\infty}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to\mathcal{E}
, the composition

\phi\circ\sigma^{\infty}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is still
a parametrized family of formal solutions.

(d) Denote by
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
the category whose
objects are formally integrable generalized PDEs
\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
, with
\mathcal{E},Y\in\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
, and whose morphisms are

\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-morphisms
\phi\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
that preserve formal solutions.

Remark 3.48
.

(a)
Definition
3.47
(a) says that the prolongation of a
PDE locus
\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y

(definition
3.39
) yields another PDE locus

\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
that is
smaller or equal
to the original one.
In the sense of remark
3.45
this
means that there are in general
more
/
stronger
equations
characterizing
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
than there are equations
characterizing
\mathcal{E}
, and it is in this sense that the
differential equation is
prolonged
as we pass to

\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
.

(b) Combining the definition of the prolongation
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
of
a PDE with example
3.43
shows that both

\mathcal{E}
and its prolongation have the same true solutions,
because by definition they have the same formal solutions. But it is
important to note that dealing with formal solutions is indispensable
in this context. There are examples of PDEs that have lots of formal
solutions, but none that can be extended even to local
sections
[
18
]
(see
[
36
]
for an insightful
exposition). Thus, if we were to define the prolongation

\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
of
\mathcal{E}
as the largest sub-object that
admits the same true solutions, it would be empty in those cases.
However, under such a definition the prolongation would be much more
difficult to compute, because jets do not see past the “horizon” of
formal solutions.

(c) Finally, a comment on the term
formally integrable
. One way
construct a formal solution
\sigma^{\infty}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}(*)\simeq\mathbb{D}_{*}\to\mathcal{E}
is as a limit
\sigma^{\infty}=\varprojlim_{k}\sigma^{k}
, where
\mathbb{D}_{*}=\varinjlim_{k}\mathbb{D}_{*}(k)
and
\sigma^{k}\colon\mathbb{D}_{*}(k)\to\mathcal{E}

are formal solutions of finite orders. The construction would proceed
inductively, by starting with a
\sigma^{0}\colon{*}\to\mathcal{E}

and successively constructing higher orders. However, if the

\sigma^{0}
does not factor through the canonical inclusion

\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}
, this inductive
procedure will be obstructed at some higher order and it is said that
such a finite order formal solution is
not integrable
. Thus,
another way to interpret
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
is as the largest
sub-object of
\mathcal{E}
that consists of
0
-th order formal
solutions that are integrable
(lemma
A.2
makes this more precise by
proving that
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
parametrizes a universal family of
formal solutions). These ideas have lead to a finite order version of
the notion of formally integrable (for instance, as expressed in
Definition 7.1 of
[
9
]
), which when extended to infinite
order coincides with our definition
3.47
.

Remark 3.49
.

(a) Consider any full subcategory
\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
such that we can restrict
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
. One example is the
subcategory
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{LocProMfd}_{\downarrow\Sigma}
of locally pro-manifolds fibered over
\Sigma

(definition
3.18
).

(b) We can then denote by
\mathrm{PDE}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})=:\mathrm{PDE}(\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma})
the full subcategory
where
\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
and
\phi\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
are all morphisms in

\mathcal{C}
. It is important to note that the definition of

\mathrm{PDE}(\mathcal{C})
still refers to the larger category

\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
, which is needed to check the

\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-monomorphism property of
\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
as well as to introduce families of
formal solutions (for instance,
\mathcal{C}
itself might not
admit any manifolds with infinitesimal dimensions), to verify that
they are preserved under morphisms and to check formal integrability.

(c) The example of
\mathrm{PDE}_{\downarrow\Sigma}(\mathrm{LocProMfd}):=\mathrm{PDE}(\mathrm{LocProMfd}_{\downarrow\Sigma})
was explicitly
considered in
[
23
,
24
]
where it was identified with a
subcategory of Vinogradov’s category of
PDEs
[
32
,
33
]
. Strictly speaking, morphisms in
Vinogradov’s category are defined differently: instead of preserving
formal solutions they preserve an alternative geometric structure (the

Cartan distribution
). On the other hand, informal comments in
the literature (such as those at the top of
[
32
, §8.6]
) indicate that our definition is the intuitively
preferred one. However, since infinitesimals are not strictly part of
classical differential geometry, the difficulty of describing formal
solutions in a precise and concise way has made the Cartan
distribution a favored proxy in the existing literature. We will not
go into the details here of how the Cartan distribution relates to
formal solutions. It suffices to note that our definition of the PDE
category ultimately coincides with Vinogradov’s
(remark
3.54
and
corollary
3.55
), at least in the

\mathrm{PDE}_{\downarrow\Sigma}(\mathrm{LocProMfd})
case. Later, in
remark
3.59
, we will comment on how
identifying the coalgebra structure underlying a PDE could be
interpreted as putting the PDE in “canonical form.”

Next we prove some lemmas containing the main technical results needed
to establish our main theorem
3.52
below on the structure of
the category of generalized PDEs.

Lemma 3.50
.

Consider
E,E^{\prime},\mathcal{E},\in\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
, a generalized
PDE
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
(definition

3.39
) and an
E
-parametrized family of formal solutions

s_{E}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to\mathcal{E}
. Then, for any morphism

\phi\colon E^{\prime}\to E
, the composite
s_{E}\circ T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\phi\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E^{\prime}\to\mathcal{E}
is also an
E^{\prime}
-parametrized
family of formal solutions.

Proof.

The only property that we need to check is that the composition
e_{Y}\circ s_{E}\circ T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\phi\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E^{\prime}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
is an
E^{\prime}
-parametrized family of formally
holonomic sections. This property is precisely captured by the
commutativity of the outer part of the following diagram:

Here the square on the right commutes by hypothesis; the left square
commutes by naturality of the unit of the monad
T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
; while
the top triangle commutes by definition. Finally, the bottom triangle
commutes by the natural hom-isomorphism of the
(T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\dashv J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
-adjunction, which provides us with a bijection between
the following kinds of commutative triangles:

81.44952pt\raise-15.83778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\leftrightarrow}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

∎

Proposition 3.51
.

Consider a formally integrable generalized PDE
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
(definition
3.47
).

(a) Using the canonical inclusion isomorphism,
e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{E}
, a formally integrable PDE parametrizes its own universal
family of formal solutions,
\overline{\rho_{\mathcal{E}}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
, where
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}=\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}

(cf. lemma
A.2
).

(b) The adjunct morphism
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
to

\overline{\rho_{\mathcal{E}}}
defines a coalgebra structure
(definition
B.29
) over the jet comonad
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
.

(c) Conversely, any coalgebra structure
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
is also a formally
integrable generalized PDE.

(d) For
e^{\prime}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}Y^{\prime}
another
formally integrable generalized PDE, an

\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-morphism
\phi\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
preserves formal solutions iff it is a morphism of
corresponding coalgebras, meaning that it fits into the commutative
diagram

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Proof.

(a)
This is just a restatement (placed here for convenience) of the result
of lemma
A.2
in light of the
definition of formal integrability
(definition
3.47
(b)).

(b)
First to see the counitality condition, let us identify

\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\simeq\mathcal{E}
and
e_{Y}\simeq e_{Y}^{\infty}
by
formal integrability, and consider the diagram

22.04042pt\raise-67.67635pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{e_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

Here the top square is the defining pullback square of the formally
integrable PDE, while the bottom square is the naturality square for
the counit of the jet comonad. The right vertical composite is the
identity by counitality of the coproduct
\Delta
. A consequence of
the commutativity of the whole diagram is the factorization identity

e_{Y}\circ(\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{E}})=e_{Y}
. But,
since
e_{Y}
is a monomorphism and
e_{Y}\circ\mathrm{id}=e_{Y}
is
another factorization, the two factorizing morphisms must be the same,

\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{E}}=\mathrm{id}\,,

which is the counitality condition for a coalgebra structure

\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
. It also means that
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
is a split
monomorphism with retraction morphism
\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Now, to see the coaction property, consider the diagram

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\Delta_{Y}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{Y}}

made from naturality squares of the coproduct
\Delta
(it consists
of two faces of the “commuting cube” diagram
in
[
23
, §§2.3–4]
). Since

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
is a right adjoint it preserves fiber products. By
definition of a formally integrable PDE, the fiber product over the
bottom arrows is
\mathcal{E}
, and since
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
preserves
limits the fiber product over the top morphisms is
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
. By the universal property of the latter fiber product,
there is hence a unique dashed morphism
e
making the two rectangles
in the following diagram commute:

where we have also added the triangle on the left, which commutes by
the counitality of
\Delta
, and have also noted that the two
left-most horizontal morphisms compose to the identity, by the
counitality of
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
just established above. Hence, from
commutativity of the left part of this diagram, we can conclude that

e=\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\,.

With this identification, the commutativity of the left square is
exactly the coaction property of
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
.

(c)
Suppose that
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
gives
\mathcal{E}
a coalgebra structure. Then, by the
counitality condition,
\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{E}}=\mathrm{id}
, meaning that
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
is a split monomorphism
(in
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
in either case), in the same way as we
concluded in part (a). Now, the Beck equalizer theorem
(proposition
B.30
) says that
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
is an
equalizer of the diagram

Recall the canonical the canonical inclusion

(definition

) of
the prolongation
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
of
\mathcal{E}
. The pullback
diagram defining

(lemma

) can be combined with
the Beck equalizer diagram to give the following commutative diagram:

75.16264pt\raise-68.93246pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\rho_{\mathcal{E}}}$}}}\kern where we have illustrated by a dashed arrow the unique monomorphism
that factors
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
through
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
(note that
monomorphisms always factor through monomorphisms), which exists by
the pullback property. Composing the morphisms
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
gives a
factorization of the cone with vertex
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
through
itself. By the pullback property, there is only one way for such a
factorization to be done, which hence must coincide with the identity

\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
. If the composition of two monomorphisms is the
identity, then they both had to be isomorphisms to begin with. Thus,

\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
and we have shown that

\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
is a generalized formally integrable PDE.

(d)
In one direction, suppose that
\phi\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
preserves formal solutions. Then, using formal
integrability and lemma
A.2
, we
get the commutative diagram

which expresses the fact that the composition of
\phi
with the
universal family of formal solutions of
\mathcal{E}
factors through
the universal family of solutions of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
. By the

T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\dashv J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
adjunction, we get precisely the
commutative diagram showing that
\phi
is a morphism of
corresponding coalgebras.

In the other direction, reversing the preceding argument, applying the
adjunction to the commutative square showing that
\phi
is a morphism
of coalgebras, we get the commutative square with the
T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\phi
morphism as illustrated above, implying the identity
\phi\circ\overline{\rho_{\mathcal{E}}}=\overline{\rho_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}\circ T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\phi
. By lemma
3.50
, this
means that
\phi
preserves the universal family of formal solutions

\overline{\rho_{\mathcal{E}}}
and hence preserves all families of
formal solutions.
∎

Now the main theorem:

Theorem 3.52
.

Let
\mathbf{H}
be a differentially cohesive topos (definition
2.15
)
and let
\Sigma
be a
V
-manifold in
\mathbf{H}
(definition
3.3
).
Then there is an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\simeq\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})

between the category of formally integrable generalized PDEs in
\mathbf{H}

(definition
3.47
(d)) and the Eilenberg-Moore category
of coalgebras (definition
B.29
) over the jet comonad
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\colon\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}\to\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
(definition
3.23
), all over
\Sigma
. Moreover,
for any full subcategory
\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
, the equivalence restricts to the corresponding
subcategories
\mathrm{PDE}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}|_{\mathcal{C}})
, meaning that the
following diagram commutes:

0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-27.11115pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.85942pt\raise-27.21591pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.60422pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 55.60422pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

Proof.

Proposition
3.51
shows that there are functors in both directions
between
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
and

\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
. It remains only to check that they
compose to identity, up to natural isomorphism, in both directions. It
is obvious from part (c) that the coalgebra
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}
gets sent back to itself
under composition in one direction. In the other direction, a formally
integrable PDE
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y

gets sent back to
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
. However, the natural morphism

\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
clearly
preserves formal solutions and is an isomorphism. Hence, we have the
desired equivalence of categories.

Finally, consider a full subcategory
\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
that is closed under
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
.
If
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{C}
, then all the morphisms needed
to define a PDE or coalgebra structure on it are also

\mathcal{C}
, because it is a full subcategory. Similarly, any
morphism in
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
or

\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
breaks down to morphisms in

\mathcal{C}
if its source and target have underlying objects
in
\mathcal{C}
. Hence, clearly,

\mathrm{PDE}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
and

\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}|_{\mathcal{C}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
are full subcategories that are respected
by the equivalence.
∎

Remark 3.53
.

We introduced the notion of a PDE (definition
3.39
) by
relying on the extrinsic structure of its inclusion
\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
in a jet bundle, which also plays a
crucial role in the corresponding notion of a solution. While this
extrinsic structure seems natural from the traditional point of view on
PDEs, a complete equivalence of the category of PDEs based on this
definition (definition
3.47
(d)) with coalgebras
over the
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
comonad shows that this extrinsic information
is not actually necessary and the only inclusion that counts is that of
a (formally integrable) PDE
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
in its own jet bundle, with

\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
precisely giving it the structure of a coalgebra.
This completely intrinsic structure can be still seen as a traditional
PDE when written out in the following way:

j^{\infty}\phi=\rho_{\mathcal{E}}(\phi)\,,

where
\phi\colon\Sigma\to\mathcal{E}
is a section. In a sense, this
form can be seen as the canonical form of a PDE achieved by “solving
for the highest derivatives,” in analogy with how it is commonly done
for ordinary differential equations. Of course, any such equation may
have integrability conditions, following from the differential
consequences of the equation as written above. From this point of view,
the coaction identity (definition
B.29
) satisfied by

\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
is a necessary and sufficient condition for the
absence of non-trivial integrability conditions. That is, plugging the
above equation into the universal integrability condition
j^{\infty}j^{\infty}\phi=\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}(j^{\infty}\phi)
implies the identity

p^{\infty}\rho_{\mathcal{E}}=\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
, but
it does not yield any new conditions on the section
\phi
, because

p^{\infty}\rho_{\mathcal{E}}=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}
by definition
3.34
and

\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
already satisfies the identity
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}=\Delta_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
by virtue of being defining a coalgebra structure on

\mathcal{E}
.

Remark 3.54
.

As mentioned earlier in remark
3.49
(c), while
our definition for the category of generalized PDEs shares the same
heuristic foundations with the one in Vinogradov’s
approach
[
32
,
33
]
, they are a priori distinct,
though ours could be said to be intuitively closer to these
heuristics. Both have an intrinsic description, without requiring an
extrinsic embedding into a jet bundle, ours in terms of jet coalgebra
structures and Vinogradov’s in terms of the Cartan distribution. We
are finally at a point where we can state a precise relation between
these two categories. Instead of relating the two definitions
directly, which would require a detailed synthetic formalization of
the notion of the Cartan distribution, we will simply rely on Marvan’s
original and insightful observation
[
23
,
24
]
of the
equivalence of Vinogradov’s category with the Eilenberg-Moore
category of coalgebras over the jet comonad in fibered manifolds
(definition
3.18
), which by our main
theorem
3.52
embeds fully and faithfully in our category of
generalized PDEs in the Cahiers topos
\mathbf{H}=\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}
.

Corollary 3.55
.

Vinogradov’s category of PDEs over an ordinary manifold
\Sigma

embeds fully and faithfully in

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet})
, with image

\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}|_{\mathrm{LocProMfd}_{\downarrow\Sigma}})
.

Example 3.56
.

There are several immediate sources of examples of morphisms in the PDE
category.

(a) Recall that the co-Kleisli category of cofree coalgebras over a
comonad (definition
B.34
) embeds as a full subcategory of
the Eilenberg-Moore category of coalgebras over a comonad
(definition
B.29
) and, in
particular, we have

\mathrm{Kl}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
.
Putting together the notation from
definition
3.34
and the equivalences

\mathrm{DiffOp}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\simeq\mathrm{Kl}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})

(proposition
3.37
) and

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\simeq\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
, we
have the full embedding

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 31.41672pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-31.41672pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{DiffOp}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.41673pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.25058pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma},p^{\infty})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.41672pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 55.41672pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 85.41672pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,,

where the notation means that objects are embedded as
cofree
PDEs
,
E\mapsto(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta_{E}}}{{\longrightarrow}}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E)
, and morphisms as infinitely prolonged
differential operators,
(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}E_{2})\mapsto(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{\infty}\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E_{2})
. Since the
embedding is full (remark
B.36
), it means that
the only allowed morphisms between cofree PDEs are prolonged
differential operators.

(b) The trivial bundle
[\Sigma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma]\in\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
embeds in
\mathrm{DiffOp}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})

and hence as a cofree object
\Sigma\in\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
. Recall that
\Sigma\simeq J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\Sigma
(example
3.26
). Hence, every
PDE morphism
\sigma^{\infty}\colon\Sigma\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
into a
cofree object must be of the form
\sigma^{\infty}=p^{\infty}\sigma=j^{\infty}\sigma
, for some section
\sigma\colon\Sigma\to Y
.

(c) The inclusion morphism of a formally integrable generalized PDE in
a jet bundle,
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y

is another example of a morphism in
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
.
Keeping in mind the isomorphism
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\simeq\mathcal{E}
,
this can be seen directly from the top square of the commutative
diagram in lemma
A.2
, which
precisely shows that
e_{Y}
is a morphism of coalgebras.

(d) For any full subcategory
\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
, such as
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{LocProMfd}_{\downarrow\Sigma}
, to which we can restrict the

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
comonad, all of the following embeddings of categories
are compatible:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern Theorem 3.57

(Products and equalizers)
.

Consider two formally integrable PDEs
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
, presented as coalgebras over

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
with coaction morphisms
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
and
\rho_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{F}
.

(a) The
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-product
5
5

5

Since
\Sigma\in\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
is the terminal
object, the product in
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
must be computed as
the
\mathcal{E}\times_{\Sigma}\mathcal{F}
product in
\mathbf{H}
.

\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{F}
has the coalgebra structure

\rho_{\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{F}}=(\rho_{\mathcal{E}},\rho_{\mathcal{F}})\colon\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{F}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}\times J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{F}\simeq J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{F})\,,

which makes it into the
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
-product of

\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{F}
.

(b)
For any pair of coalgebra morphisms
f,g\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F}
, the
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-equalizer

\textstyle{\mathcal{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}}

has a unique coalgebra structure,
\rho_{\mathcal{K}}\colon\mathcal{K}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{K}
, that makes
e\colon\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{E}
into a morphism of coalgebras and moreover the

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
-equalizer of
f
and
g
.

(c)
When,
\mathcal{E}=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
,
\mathcal{F}=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Z

and
f=p^{\infty}f_{0}
,
g=p^{\infty}g_{0}
for some differential operators

f,g\colon J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y\to Z
. The

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
-equalizer
\mathcal{K}
of
p^{\infty}f_{0}
and
p^{\infty}g_{0}
coincides with the prolongation of the

\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-equalizer

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.13196pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.13196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.13197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.96925pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{e_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.13196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.13196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern That is,
\mathcal{K}\simeq(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}

(lemma
A.2
), as
formally integrable generalized PDEs.

Proof.

(a)
This part follows directly from the observation that
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}

preserves
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
-products.

(b)
Given that
e=\ker_{\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}}(f,g)\colon\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{E}
, it is a monomorphism. Since equalizers are preserved by

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
(from definition
3.23
it is right adjoint and
hence preserves all limits),
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e\colon J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{K}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
is also a monomorphism. By the
properties of morphisms of coalgebras, the solid arrows in the following
diagram in
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
commute:

And, because
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e
is a monomorphism, there exists the dashed
arrow
\rho_{\mathcal{K}}
that is unique in making the whole diagram
commute. It follows that the left square in
the diagram is a pullback square for
\mathcal{K}
. Pasting it together
with the coaction square for
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
(which is also a
pullback square, by Proposition
3.52
(c)), we get the diagram

whose outer square is hence also a pullback square for
\mathcal{K}
, by the
pasting law (Proposition
B.1
). In
other words
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\circ e\colon\mathcal{K}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
is a formally integrable PDE and hence, by
proposition
3.52
(b), the morphism

\rho_{\mathcal{K}}\colon\mathcal{K}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{K}
endows

\mathcal{K}
it with the structure of a coalgebra. The commutativity of
the left square of the above diagram then means that
e\colon\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{E}
is a morphism of coalgebras, with

\rho_{\mathcal{K}}
the unique coalgebra structure making it true.

It remains to show that any other other coalgebra morphism
h\colon\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{E}
that equalizes
f
and
g
must uniquely
factor through
\mathcal{K}
. Since both
e
and
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e
are
equalizers in
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
, there exists dashed arrow
u
that
is unique in making the top loop in the following diagram commute, with
the bottom loop also commuting since it is the
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
image of
the top one:

We already know that the right square and the outer square commute.
Hence, we can conclude that

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e\circ(\rho_{\mathcal{K}}\circ u)=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e\circ(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}u\circ\rho_{\mathcal{H}})\,.

But, since
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e
is a monomorphism, it must be true that

\rho_{\mathcal{K}}\circ u=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}u\circ\rho_{\mathcal{K}}
and
therefore that the left square commutes as well. This shows that

u\colon\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{K}
is a morphism of coalgebras.
Therefore, we have shown that any
h
as above must uniquely factor
through
e
in
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
, we can conclude that

e=\ker_{\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})}(f,g)
.

(c)
Consider the following diagram:

64.76746pt\raise-63.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.72726pt\raise-69.4186pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.40288pt\raise-63.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.40288pt\raise-63.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 131.90039pt\raise-54.06747pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}f_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 157.38902pt\raise-60.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 132.1662pt\raise-72.59915pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}g_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 157.38902pt\raise-66.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 157.38902pt\raise-63.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Z}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

The bottom line is an equalizer, since
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
preserves
equalizers, while the inner square is the defining pullback square of
the prolongation
(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}
. Since
e
is the equalizer of

p^{\infty}f_{0}=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}f_{0}\circ\Delta_{Y}
and
p^{\infty}g_{0}=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}g_{0}\circ\Delta_{Y}
,
\Delta_{Y}\circ e
equalizes

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}f_{0}
and
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}g_{0}
. Hence
\Delta_{Y}\circ e

uniquely factors through
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{K}_{0}
, illustrated by
the dotted monomorphism above (recall that monomorphisms always factor
through monomorphisms), which hence commutes with all the solid arrows.
This commutativity and the pullback property of
(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}

implies the unique commuting factorization
\mathcal{K}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}
, illustrated by one of the dashed morphisms above.
On the other hand, the commutativity of the pullback square implies that

(e_{0})^{\infty}
equalizes
p^{\infty}f_{0}
and
p^{\infty}g_{0}
, which implies the
unique commuting factorization
(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}
, illustrated by the other dashed morphism above.

Now, composing the two dashed morphisms as
(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}
gives a
commuting factorization of
(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}
through itself, which
by uniqueness of commuting factorizations through a pullback must be
equal to the identity
(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}
. But if the
composition of two monomorphisms is the identity, they must have both
been isomorphisms to begin with. Thus,
e\simeq(e_{0})^{\infty}
and

\mathcal{K},(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
are
equivalent subobjects. Since each of them can be uniquely endowed with
the structure of a coalgebra such that the monomorphisms
e
and

(e_{0})^{\infty}
are morphisms of coalgebras,
\mathcal{K}
and

(\mathcal{K}_{0})^{\infty}
are also isomorphic as objects in

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
.
∎

If all pairwise products and equalizers exist, then all finite products
and equalizers exist. Moreover, it is well-known that any category with
a terminal object, finite products and finite equalizers has all finite
limits
[
21
, cor.V.2.1]
. Hence, from parts (a) and (b) of the
preceding theorem
3.57
we have the

Corollary 3.58
.

The category
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
has all finite limits
and they can be computed as
\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
limits.

We conclude this section by underscoring one of the central advantages
of the equivalence
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\simeq\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})

that we have established in theorem
3.52
. Namely with this equivalence at hand, we
may precisely characterize solutions of a PDE using only its intrinsic
coalgebra structure, this is proposition
3.59

below.

Proposition 3.59
.

Given a formally integrable generalized PDE
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
over
\Sigma

(definition
3.47
), the
induced
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
-coalgebra
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}

(proposition
3.52
) has the property that
coalgebra morphisms from
\Sigma
(regarded as a jet coalgebra via
example
3.56
(b)) into it are in natural bijection
with solutions to the PDE according to definition
3.39
:

\mathrm{Sol}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{E})\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})}(\Sigma,\mathcal{E})\,.

Proof.

Suppose that
\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sigma\to\mathcal{E}
is
a morphism in
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
. By
example
3.56
(c), so is
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
. Hence, so is
\sigma^{\infty}_{Y}=e_{Y}\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sigma\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
. But now, by
example
3.56
(b), any such morphism must be of the form

\sigma^{\infty}_{Y}=j^{\infty}\sigma_{Y}
for some section
\sigma_{Y}\colon\Sigma\to Y
. Noticing now that
j^{\infty}\sigma_{Y}=e_{Y}\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
immediately implies that
\sigma_{Y}\in\mathrm{Sol}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{E})
.

In the other direction, suppose that
\sigma_{Y}\colon\Sigma\to Y
has
a jet extension that factors through
\mathcal{E}
, that is
j^{\infty}\sigma_{Y}=e_{Y}\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
for some

\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\Sigma\to\mathcal{E}
. Hence, it is
easy to see that the following is a pullback diagram in

\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.68059pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.58751pt\raise 6.0853pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.7118pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.68059pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.7118pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 45.1146pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{e_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.1146pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 8.72548pt\raise-37.59915pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}\sigma_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.09888pt\raise-15.75693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.25pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{\tiny(pb)}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

By corollary
3.58
, the same diagram is also a
pullback diagram in
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\simeq\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
, meaning that
\sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})}(\Sigma,\mathcal{E})
.
∎

3.7
The topos of synthetic PDEs

We have seen above in section
3.5
(theorem
3.52
) that for
\Sigma
a
V
-manifold, the Eilenberg-Moore
category (definition
B.29
)
of coalgebras for the jet comonad (definition
3.23
), acting on
any object of a differentially cohesive topos
\mathbf{H}
(definition
2.15
)
is equivalently the category of formally integrable generalized PDEs,
generalized in the sense that both their
underlying bundles as well as their solution loci may again be arbitrary objects of
\mathbf{H}
:

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\simeq\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})\,.

It is a general fact that for
J
a comonad, such that

1.

J
acts on an elementary topos,

2.

J
is a right adjoint,

the Eilenberg-Moore category
\mathrm{EM}(J)
is itself an
elementary topos
[
22
, V8, cor. 7]
. Here we show
(theorem
3.63
below) that at least for
\mathbf{H}=\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}

then
\mathrm{PDE}_{\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})

is in fact a category of sheaves (definition
B.18
) over the category of “ordinary” PDEs, i.e., of PDEs
in the category of (infinitesimally thickened) fibered manifolds.

This result shows that the generalized formally integrable PDEs of definition
3.47

are related to ordinary PDEs in the same way that the (formal) smooth sets of definition
2.1

and definition
2.11
are related to (formal) smooth manifolds.
In particular this implies that every PDE in the generalized sense is but a colimit of PDEs in the
ordinary sense. In a followup we will show that this may be used in order to identify
a synthetic formulation for the variational bicomplex
[
1
]
construction
(which lies at the foundation of variational calculus and Lagrangian
field theory) inside
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})
.

Let throughout this section
\mathbf{H}:=\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}:=\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{FormalCartSp})
be the
Cahiers topos (definition
2.11
) with
\Im\colon\mathbf{H}\to\mathbf{H}
denoting the infinitesimal shape monad from
proposition
2.15
, and the natural
transformation
\eta\colon\mathrm{id}\to\Im
its unit.

Theorem 3.60
.

Let
\Sigma\in\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}
be any object.
Then the functor

(\eta_{\Sigma})^{\ast}\;\colon\;\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}_{/\Im\Sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})\simeq\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet})

(from the slice over
\Im\Sigma
to the Eilenberg-Moore category of
theorem
3.52
) which equips the pullback
(\eta_{\Sigma})^{\ast}E

with the coalgebra structure given by

\textstyle{(\eta_{\Sigma})^{\ast}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\eta_{\Sigma})(\epsilon_{E})}
\textstyle{(\eta_{\Sigma})^{\ast}(\eta_{\Sigma})_{\ast}(\eta_{\Sigma})^{\ast}E=J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\left((\eta_{\Sigma})^{*}E\right)}

(where
\epsilon\colon\mathrm{id}\to(\eta_{\Sigma})_{\ast}(\eta_{\Sigma})^{\ast}
is the unit of the adjunction
(\eta_{\Sigma})^{\ast}\dashv(\eta_{\Sigma})_{\ast}
) is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

By proposition
B.41
it is sufficient that

(\eta_{\Sigma})\colon\Sigma\longrightarrow\Im\Sigma
is an
epimorphism. This is the case by proposition

2.18
.
∎

Proposition 3.61
.

For
\Sigma\in\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}
any object, there is an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{FormalCartSp}/\Im\Sigma)

which identifies the category of generalized PDEs
(definition
3.47
(d)) with the
category of sheaves (definition
B.18
) over the slice site
(proposition
B.25
) of that of formal Cartesian spaces
(definition
2.11
) over
\Sigma
.

Proof.

This follows via theorem
3.60

by proposition
B.25
.
∎

By theorem
3.60
we may
identify the objects in the slice site
\mathrm{FormalCartSp}/\Im\Sigma
of proposition

3.61
as generalized PDEs. But in fact these turn out
to be just very mildly generalized, in that their solution locus is a
locally pro-manifold that may admit a formal infinitesimal thickening:

Proposition 3.62
.

For
\Sigma\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H}:=\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}
a smooth
manifold, the equivalence of theorem

3.60
restricted to the
objects in the image under the Yoneda embedding of the slice site of
proposition
3.61
factors through jet coalgebra
structures (definition
B.29
) in formal locally
pro-manifolds (definition
2.33
). That is, there
exists a dashed arrow that makes the following diagram commute:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 41.9376pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-41.9376pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 41.9376pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 95.9376pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.30675pt\raise-15.97223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.9376pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 223.72102pt\raise 4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 240.63948pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 240.63948pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 303.40352pt\raise-8.41664pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 303.40352pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 133.92392pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 223.72102pt\raise-36.673pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 276.29237pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 276.29237pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

Proof.

By theorem
3.60
the bottom left equivalence in the
above diagram sends an object

X=\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}\in\mathrm{FormalCartSp}

in the slice over
\Im\Sigma

[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Im\Sigma]\in\mathrm{FormalCartSp}_{/\Im\Sigma}

to its pullback along
\Sigma\overset{\eta_{\Sigma}}{\to}\Im\Sigma
into the slice over
\Sigma

[\eta_{\Sigma}^{*}X\longrightarrow\Sigma]\in\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}_{/\Sigma}

and equipped there with some coalgebra structure. This coalgebra structure is
of no concern for the proof, all we need to check is that this pullback actually lands in the
image of the inclusion

\mathrm{FormalLocProMfd}_{\downarrow\Sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}_{/\Sigma}\,.

To this end, we claim that:

Claim:
Every morphism of the form

f\colon X\longrightarrow\Im\Sigma

for
X=\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}\in\mathrm{FormalCartSp}

factors as

X\overset{f^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta_{\Sigma}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Im\Sigma\,.

With this claim the desired statement follows:
By the pasting law (proposition
B.1
) the pullback of any

f
that is so factored is given by
the pasting of two pullbacks as in the following diagram

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise-62.72218pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 17.09874pt\raise-67.90967pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta_{\Sigma}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.38716pt\raise-62.72218pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.38716pt\raise-62.72218pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Im\Sigma}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

This identifies the pullback with the infinitesimal disk bundle from definition

3.8

\eta_{\Sigma}^{\ast}X\simeq T_{\Sigma}^{\infty}X

as shown. Moreover, by example
3.4
and proposition
3.13
,
the object
T^{\infty}\Sigma
is locally a Cartesian product of a chart

\mathbb{R}^{n}\hookrightarrow\Sigma

with the formal neighbourhood
\mathbb{D}^{n}
of any point in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and according to corollary
3.16

the morphism
\mathrm{ev}
is given on generalized elements
(definition
3.15
)
by
(s,d)\mapsto s-d
. Therefore
T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}X
is locally
the pullback in the following diagram:

\textstyle{{X|_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}}\times\mathbb{D}^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(x,d)\mapsto x}
\scriptstyle{(x,d)\mapsto f^{\prime}(x)+d}
(pb)
\textstyle{X|_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x\mapsto f^{\prime}(x)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(s,d)\mapsto s-d}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{n}}

Now since
{X|_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}}\times\mathbb{D}^{n}
is the Cartesian product of
a formal manifold with an infinitesimally thickened point, it is itself a formal manifold
and so in particular a formal locally pro-manifold
according to definition
2.33
. This is the desired conclusion.

It remains to prove the above claim that every
f
factors through
f^{\prime}
in this
way. To that end, recall the adjoint quadruple

i_{!}\dashv i^{\ast}\dashv i_{\ast}\dashv i^{!}\;:\;\mathrm{SmoothSet}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}

from proposition
2.14
and the
identification
\Im=i_{\ast}i^{\ast}
from definition

2.15
. Using this and forming adjuncts,
(definition
B.4
) the morphisms in
\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}
of the form
f:X\to\Im\Sigma
are in natural bijection with morphisms in
\mathrm{SmoothSet}
of the form

\phi:i^{\ast}X\longrightarrow i^{\ast}\Sigma\,.

Here
i^{\ast}X=\mathbb{R}^{k}
is just the ordinary Cartesian space
underlying the formal Cartesian space
X=\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}
, and
i^{\ast}\Sigma
is just the smooth manifold
\Sigma
itself, regarded in

\mathrm{SmoothSet}
. Now if we denote by
\tilde{f}
the composite

\tilde{f}:X\to\Re X=i_{!}i^{\ast}X\overset{i_{!}\phi}{\longrightarrow}i_{!}i^{\ast}\Sigma=\Sigma

then

\phi=i^{\ast}\tilde{f}

by the fact that
i_{!}
is fully faithful, and using proposition
B.6
.

Now by the formula for adjuncts (proposition
B.8
)
the fact that
\phi
is, by definition, the
(i^{\ast}\dashv i_{\ast})
-adjunct of
f
means that the
left triangle in the following diagram commutes

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.56818pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.77322pt\raise-15.6798pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.46397pt\raise-23.97372pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.33748pt\raise 6.61111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.61943pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 54.23055pt\raise-15.6798pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta_{\Sigma}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.23055pt\raise-23.97372pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-15.57707pt\raise-31.35959pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{i_{\ast}i^{\ast}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.48904pt\raise-41.55893pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.57707pt\raise-31.35959pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{i_{\ast}i^{\ast}\Sigma}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

But the equality which we just established, shown on the bottom edge of the diagram, now exhibits
this triangle as being one half of the naturality square of the unit
\eta:\mathrm{id}\to i_{\ast}i^{\ast}

on
\tilde{f}
. The other half is the right triangle shown in the above diagram. Therefore this
right triangle also commutes, and this is the factorization to be shown.
∎

This now allows us to use the category of ordinary PDEs as a site that
presents the category of generalized PDEs:

Theorem 3.63
.

For
\Sigma\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet}

a smooth manifold, then there
is a subcanonical coverage (definition
B.20
) on the category
\mathrm{PDE}_{\downarrow\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalLocProMfd})
of formally integrable PDEs in
formal manifolds (definition
2.33
), making it
a site (definition
B.17
), such that there is an equivalence of
categories

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalLocProMfd}))

which identifies the category of generalized formally integrable PDEs
\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathbf{H})\simeq\mathrm{EM}(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma})
from theorem
3.52
with the
category of sheaves (definition
B.18
) over the category of PDEs in the ordinary sense.

Proof.

By proposition
3.61
there is an equivalence of
categories

\mathrm{PDE}_{/\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalSmoothSet})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{FormalCartSp}/\Im\Sigma)

with sheaves on the slice site of formal Cartesian spaces over
\Im\Sigma
. By
proposition
3.62
there
is a full inclusion

\mathrm{FormalCartSp}/\Im\Sigma\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PDE}_{\downarrow\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalLocProMfd})

of the slice site into the category of ordinary PDEs. Moreover, since

\mathrm{FormalCartSp}/\Im\Sigma
is a site of definition for PDEs,
the objects in
\mathrm{PDE}_{\downarrow\Sigma}(\mathrm{FormalLocProMfd})
are faithfully tested on

\mathrm{FormalCartSp}/\Im\Sigma
. Thus
proposition
B.26
implies the
claim.
∎

Appendix A
Formal solutions of PDEs

We state and prove here two technical lemmas regarding the general
concept of formal solutions of PDEs (definition
3.40
).

In order to prevent an explosion of notation, we will use the
following device in the sequel. Since the
T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\dashv J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
adjunction provides us with a natural bijection

\mathrm{Hom}(T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}(-),-)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(-,J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}(-))
(definition
B.4
), we may freely use one kind of morphism to label the other kind.
For instance, recalling the notation for adjunct morphisms introduced in
definition
3.23
, we might use
\overline{\alpha}
to refer
to an arbitrary morphism
T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to Y
, which is labelled by
the corresponding morphism
\alpha\colon E\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
.

Lemma A.1
.

Consider
E,\mathcal{E},Y\in\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
, together with a
monomorphism
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
.

(a) For an
E
-parametrized family of formal solutions

\overline{\rho^{E}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to\mathcal{E}
, the morphism

\rho^{E}\colon E\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
fits into a commutative
diagram of the following form

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 39.58585pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.97916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.04042pt\raise 6.72002pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.71446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{e^{E}_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.38197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-15.63889pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5367pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho^{E}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise where

1.

e^{E}_{\mathcal{E}}:=\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho^{E}
as shown;

2.

e^{E}_{Y}:=e_{Y}\circ\overline{\rho^{E}}\circ\eta_{E}
.

(b) For a pair of morphisms
\rho^{E}\colon E\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}
and

that fit into a
commutative diagram of the form

the adjunct morphism
\overline{\rho^{E}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to\mathcal{E}
is an
E
-parametrized family of formal solutions and the
above commutative square coincides with the top square of the
commutative diagram associated to
\overline{\rho^{E}}
in part (a).

Proof.

(a)
Let us start with the defining property of
\overline{\rho^{E}}
as a
family of formal solutions. Namely,
\overline{\rho^{E}}
and its adjunct

\rho^{E}
together fit into the following commutative diagram:

90.82468pt\raise-78.33328pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.08252pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.82468pt\raise-69.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

The middle square commutes because by hypothesis the composition
e_{Y}\circ\overline{\rho^{E}}
is a family of formally holonomic sections of

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
, which factors through
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
by virtue of consisting of formal
solutions. The top and bottom triangle are related by the

T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\dashv J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
adjunction, which explains the
appearance of the morphism
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y}
. The dashed diagonal
arrow, which we have chosen to denote by
e^{E}_{Y}
, is the unique one
that commutes with the rest of the diagram, namely
e^{E}_{Y}=e_{Y}\circ\overline{\rho^{E}}\circ\eta_{E}
.

This shows that the subdiagram consisting of the morphisms
\rho^{E}
,

e^{E}_{Y}
,
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y}
and
\Delta_{Y}
commutes and is
identical to the top square of the desired diagram in part (a) of the
theorem. We can now conclude that this square commutes.

The rest of
the desired diagram is constructed by pasting to it the naturality
square of the
\epsilon
counit (definition
B.27
) of

J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
(bottom), the counit-coproduct commutative triangle
(definition
B.29
.2) of
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
(right) and the
composite morphism
e^{E}_{\mathcal{E}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho^{E}
(left). Since all the pasted subdiagrams commute, the whole
diagram commutes as well, as was desired.

(b)
Let us split the commutative square from the hypothesis in part
(b) of the theorem into two triangles, with
\tau
denoting the
common morphism between them:

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

Now we argue with the method of local generalized elements as in
remark
3.41
that the diagrams shown
with dashed morphisms exist and commute: Since
\tau
is the image
of composing with the jet coproduct over a
V
-manifold, it depends on
its infinitesimal arguments symmetrically,
\tau(x)(a,b)=e^{E}_{Y}(x)(a+b)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{Y}\circ\overline{\tau}\circ\nabla_{E}(x,a,b)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{Y}\circ\overline{\tau}(x,a+b)=\overline{\tau}(x,a+b)(0)=\tau(x)(a+b,0)

\displaystyle=e^{E}_{Y}(x)(a+b)=\tau(x)(a,b)=\overline{\overline{\tau}}(x,a,b)\,.

This shows that the square on the top right commutes. On the other
hand, the commutative triangle on the left shows that

\overline{\tau}
factors through
\overline{\rho^{E}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to\mathcal{E}
. In other words, as was desired, we
have shown that
\overline{\rho^{E}}
is a family of formal solutions.

Finally, it is obvious that applying to
\overline{\rho^{E}}
the
argument of part (a) we recover the same commutative square that we
have started with.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Consider
\mathcal{E},Y\in\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
and a generalized
PDE
e_{Y}\colon\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y
.

Every parametrized family of formal solutions of this PDE factors
uniquely through a universal one,

\overline{\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{E}
, which is defined by the pullback square in the
following commutative diagram:

which defines the
e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{E}
morphism and where each of
e^{\infty}_{Y}
,

\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
is a monomorphism.

If
\overline{\rho^{E}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to\mathcal{E}
, with
E\in\mathbf{H}_{/\Sigma}
is a parametrized family of formal solutions,
then it factors through the universal family

\overline{\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}}
as illustrated in the commutative
diagram

where

is the unique morphism such

Proof.

As demonstrated in lemma
A.1
, an
equivalent way of presenting an
E
-parametrized family of formal
solutions
\overline{\rho^{E}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\to J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}Y

is by the pair of morphisms
\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
e^{E}_{Y}=\epsilon_{Y}\circ(J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y})\circ\rho^{E}
that fit into the
commutative diagram illustrated in part (b) of that proposition. But
by the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
through the pullback diagram
above and by the universality property of pullbacks, there exists a
unique morphism
\phi
(dashed below) that makes the following diagram
commute:

By the
T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\dashv J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
adjunction, it then follows
that the morphism
T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\phi
(dashed below) making the
following diagram commute:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.8316pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.8316pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.87479pt\raise-6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\phi}$}}}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 38.8316pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.3143pt\raise-5.83888pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.8143pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 99.8143pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{E}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Finally, the commutativity of the pullback square defining

\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
and lemma
A.1
(b)
imply that
\overline{\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}}\colon T^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{E}
is itself a parametrized family of
formal solutions. In other words,
\overline{\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}}
is
the desired universal family of formal solutions through which every
other one factors uniquely, in the manner indicated above.

Now we conclude by checking the monomorphism conditions. The
commutative diagram in the hypothesis is obtained by taking the
pullback square defining
\mathcal{E}^{\infty}
and pasting to it the
naturality square of the
\epsilon
counit (definition
B.27
)
of
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
(bottom), the counit-coproduct commutative triangle
(definition
B.29
.2) of
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}
(right) and the
composite morphism
e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
(left). Since all the pasted subdiagrams
commute, the whole diagram commutes as well, as was desired. Recall
that both
\Delta_{Y}
and
J^{\infty}_{\Sigma}e_{Y}
are monomorphisms, hence
their pullbacks
\rho^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
e^{\infty}_{Y}
are also
monomorphisms. The commutativity of the diagram in the hypothesis
implies the identity
e^{\infty}_{Y}=e_{Y}\circ e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Hence,
since
e^{\infty}_{Y}
is a monomorphism, its factorization map through

e_{Y}
, that is
e^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
, must also be a monomorphism.
∎

Appendix B
Category theoretic background

For reference, we collect here some standard facts from category theory
that are referred to in the main text. Unless indicated otherwise, proof
of these facts may be found in
[
21
,
3
]
.

B.1
Universal constructions

We list some basic statements about (co-)limits and Kan extensions, see for instance
[
3
, vol 1, section 2]

Proposition B.1

(pasting law, e.g.
[
3
, vol 1, prop. 2.5.9]
)
.

Consider in any category a commuting diagram of the from

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(pb)

such that the right square is Cartesian (is a pullback square). Then the left
square is Cartesian precisely if the total rectangle is.

Definition B.2

(e.g.
[
21
, III.6]
)
.

For
\mathcal{C}
a category with finite products
(hence with a terminal object
\ast
and with binary Cartesian products
(-)\times(-)
),
then a
monoid object

in
\mathcal{C}
is an object
G\in\mathcal{C}
equipped with

1.

(unit) a morphism
e:\ast\longrightarrow G

2.

(binary product) a morphism
(-)\cdot(-):G\times G\longrightarrow G
;

such that

•

(associativity) the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{G\times G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id},(-)\cdot(-))}
\scriptstyle{((-)\cdot(-),\mathrm{id})}
\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\cdot(-)}
\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\cdot(-)}
\textstyle{G}

•

(unitality) the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\ast\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{e\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\cdot(-)}
\textstyle{G}

Such a monoid object is called
commutative
if

•

the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\cdot(-)}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\cdot(-)}
\textstyle{G}

(where the top morphism exchanges the two factors
(g_{1},g_{2})\mapsto(g_{2},g_{1})
).

A monoid object is a
group object
if

•

(inverses) there exists a morphism
(-)^{-1}:G\longrightarrow G

such that

•

(invertibility) the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times(-)^{-1}}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\cdot(-)}
\textstyle{G\times G}

A monoid object that is both commutative as well as a group object is also called
an
abelian group object
.

Proposition B.3

(nonabelian Mayer-Vietoris lemma)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category with finite products, and let
G\in\mathcal{C}
be equipped with the structure of a group object (definition

B.2
). Then for
f:X\to G
and
g:Y\to G
two morphisms
in
\mathcal{C}
, their fiber product also makes the following square
Cartesian:

Definition B.4

(e.g.
[
3
, vol 1, section 3]
)
.

A pair of
adjoint functors

denoted
(L\dashv R)
(or an
adjunction
between two functors), is a pair of functors of the form

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}}

equipped with a natural isomorphism (“forming adjuncts”) between their hom-functors of the form

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(L(-),-)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(-,R(-))\,.

Here
L
is called
left adjoint to
R
and
R
is called
right adjoint to
L
.
The image
\eta_{d}
of
\mathrm{id}_{Ld}
under this isomorphism is called the
unit
of the adjunction at
d\in\mathcal{D}

\eta_{d}:d\longrightarrow R(L(d))\,,

while, conversely, the image
\epsilon_{d}
of
\mathrm{id}_{Rc}
is called the
counit

\epsilon_{d}:L(R(d))\longrightarrow d\,.

(Unit and counit are themselves natural transformations
\eta:\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\longrightarrow R\circ L

and
\epsilon:L\circ R\longrightarrow\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}
.)

One also writes horizontal lines for indicating these bijections between sets of adjunct morphisms:

d

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\longrightarrow}}

Rc

Ld

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\longrightarrow}}

c

There are various equivalent definitions of adjoint functors:

Proposition B.5
.

An adjunction
L\dashv R
between two functors (definition
B.4
)
is equivalent to two natural transformations, the unit

\eta:\mathrm{id}\longrightarrow R\circ L

and the counit

\epsilon:L\circ R\longrightarrow\mathrm{id},

such that the following “zig-zag”-identities hold:

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L(\eta)}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{L\circ R\circ L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{L}}
\textstyle{L}

and

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 6.83507pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.83507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.01207pt\raise-29.43054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 161.86761pt\raise-3.73296pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.28706pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta_{R}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.83507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.83507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\circ L\circ
