115.70699pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R(\epsilon)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 161.86964pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 131.86964pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 161.86964pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Here is a list of basic properties of adjoint functors:

Proposition B.6

(e.g.
[
3
, vol 1, prop. 3.4.1]
)
.

For a pair of adjoint functors
(L\dashv R)
(definition
B.4
) the following are equivalent

1.

the right adjoint
R
is a fully faithful functor;

2.

the adjunction counit
L\circ R\longrightarrow\mathrm{id}
is a natural isomorphism

and similarly the following are equivalent:

1.

the left adjoint
R
is a fully faithful functor;

2.

the adjunction unit
\mathrm{id}\longrightarrow R\circ L
is a natural isomorphism.

Proposition B.7

(e.g
[
21
, thm.V.5.1]
[
3
, vol 1, prop. 3.2.2]
)
.

A right adjoint functor (definition
B.4
) preserves
all small limits. Dually, a left adjoint functor preserves all small
colimits.

Proposition B.8
.

Given an adjunction
(L\dashv R)
as in definition
B.4
,
then

•

the adjunct of a morphism of the form
f:d\longrightarrow Rc
is
equivalently the composite

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.00522pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.00522pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Ld\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.21379pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L(f)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.00522pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.00522pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{LRc\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.26845pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{c}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 81.8085pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 81.8085pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{c}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces;

•

the adjunct of a morphism of the form
g:Lc\longrightarrow d

is equivalently the composite

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.16377pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.16377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{c\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.23012pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta_{c}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.16377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.16377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{RLc\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.25371pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R(g)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.96706pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.96706pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Rd}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Key examples of adjoint pairs and adjoint triples are Kan extensions:

Proposition B.9

(Kan extension, e.g.
[
3
, vol 1, section 3.7]
)
.

Given a functor
f:C\longrightarrow D
between small categories, then
the induced functor on categories of presheaves
f^{\ast}:\mathrm{PSh}(D)\longrightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(C)

(given by precomposing a presheaf with
f
) has both a left and a right adjoint (definition
B.4
), denoted

f_{!}
and
f_{\ast}
respectively, and called the operations of left and right Kan extension along
f
.

(f_{!}\dashv f^{\ast}\dashv f_{\ast}):\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 19.7785pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.7785pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PSh}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.35312pt\raise 12.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f_{!}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.7785pt\raise 6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.7785pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.51979pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.51979pt\raise-12.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f_{\ast}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.7785pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.7785pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 73.7785pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PSh}(D)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Moreover, the left Kan extension of a presheaf
A\in\mathrm{PSh}(C)
is equivalently the presheaf which to any object
d\in D

assigns the set expressed by the coend formula

(f_{!}A)(d)\simeq\int^{c\in C}\mathrm{Hom}_{D}(d,f(c))\times\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{PSh}(C)}(c,A)\,,

where on the right we are identifying
c
with the presheaf that it represents.
Explicitly, this coend gives the set of equivalence classes of pairs of morphisms

(d\to f(c),c\to A)

where two such pairs are regarded as equivalent if there is a morphism
\phi:c_{1}\to c_{2}
in
C
such that
the following two triangles commute

82.6569pt\raise-49.45836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.03188pt\raise-49.45836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 82.6569pt\raise-49.45836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{c_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.78189pt\raise-77.90034pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-60.69447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-70.4167pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-82.9167pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 38.28188pt\raise-82.9167pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

In particular the left Kan extension of a representable presheaf
y(c)
is the presheaf represented by the image under
the given functor
f
of the representing object
c
:

f_{!}(y(c))\simeq y(f(x))\,.

Remark B.10
.

For a presheaf
A
on
\mathcal{C},
the presheaf
f_{*}\left(A\right)
can also be described explicitly. Namely, for
D
an object of
\mathcal{D},
one has
f_{*}\left(A\right)\left(D\right)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Psh}\left(\mathcal{C}\right)}\left(f^{*}y\left(D\right),A\right).
This follows easily from the Yoneda lemma.

Proposition B.11
.

If
f:C\hookrightarrow D
is a fully faithful functor, then so is its left Kan extension
(proposition
B.9
)

f_{!}:\mathrm{PSh}(C)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(D)
, hence (by proposition
B.6
then
the adjunction unit
\mathrm{id}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}f^{\ast}f_{!}

is a natural isomorphism.

Definition B.12
.

Given a category
\mathcal{C}
and an object

c\in\mathcal{C}
, then the
slice category

\mathcal{C}_{/c}

has as objects the morphisms of
\mathcal{C}
into
c
, and as morphisms between these the
commuting triangles in
\mathcal{C}
of the form

Proposition B.13
.

The hom-spaces in a slice category
\mathcal{C}_{/c}
, definition
B.12

are equivalently given by the fiber product:

\mathcal{C}_{/c}(f_{1},f_{2})\simeq\mathcal{C}(a_{1},a_{2})\underset{\mathcal{C}(a_{2},c)}{\times}\{f_{2}\}

of hom-spaces in
\mathcal{C}
:

where
\tilde{f}_{2}
picks the element
f_{2}
in
\mathcal{C}(a_{2},c)
.

Example B.14
.

If
\ast\in\mathcal{C}
is a terminal object, then there is an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{C}_{/\ast}\simeq\mathcal{C}

between the slice category over
\ast
(definition
B.12
) and the original category.

Example B.15
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a category with finite limits, then for every object
c\in\mathcal{C}

the slice category
\mathcal{C}_{/c}
(definition
B.12
) has terminal object given by

[c\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}c]

and with Cartesian product given by the fiber product over
c
in
\mathcal{C}
:

[a\to c]\times[b\to c]\;\simeq\;[a\times_{c}b\to c]\,.

Example B.16
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a category thought of as a category of spaces
(as in section
2
) then for
\Sigma\in\mathcal{C}
any object,
thought of as a base space, we may think of the slice category
\mathcal{C}_{/\Sigma}

(definition
B.12
) as the
category of bundles
over
\Sigma
,
in the generality where bundles are not required to be fiber bundles and in particular may have empty fibers.
For

[E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma]\in\mathcal{C}_{/\Sigma}

any such bundle, then bundle morphisms from the terminal bundle

[\Sigma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma]\in\mathcal{C}_{/\Sigma}

are equivalently sections of the bundle
E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\Sigma
. We write

\Gamma_{\Sigma}(E):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}_{/\Sigma}}\left([\Sigma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma]\;,\;[E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma]\right)

B.2
Categories of sheaves

For reference, we collect here some facts about categories of sheaves (Grothendieck toposes) that we use in the main text,
see for instance
[
3
, vol 3]

Definition B.17

(site)
.

For
\mathcal{C}
a small category, then a
coverage
or

Grothendieck pre-topology
on
\mathcal{C}
is for each
object
X\in\mathcal{C}
a set of families of morphisms

\{U_{i}\overset{\phi_{i}}{\longrightarrow}X\}_{i\in I}
into
X
,
called the
covering families
, which are such that for each morphism

Y\longrightarrow X
there exists a covering family

\{V_{i}\overset{\psi_{j}}{\longrightarrow}Y\}_{j\in J}
such that for each

i\in I
there is a commuting square of the form

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.11212pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.67421pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V_{j(i)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.11212pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.13611pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi_{j(i)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.67421pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.67421pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.29787pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.29787pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.01389pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.76315pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.76315pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

A small category
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a coverage is called a
site
.

Definition B.18
.

For
\mathcal{S}
a small site (definition
B.17
)
then a presheaf
F\colon\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Set}

is called a
sheaf
if for all covering families
\{U_{i}\overset{\phi_{i}}{\longrightarrow}U\}_{i\in I}

in
\mathcal{S}
and for all tuples of elements
(x_{i}\in F(U_{i}))_{i\in I}

and for all pairs of morphisms
g\colon V\to U_{i_{1}}
and
h\colon V\to U_{i_{2}}

in
\mathcal{S}
such that
\phi_{i_{1}}\circ g=\phi_{i_{2}}\circ h
and such that

F(g)(x_{i_{1}})=F(h)(x_{i_{2}})
then there exists a unique
x\in F(U)
such that

x_{i}=F(\phi_{i})(x)
for all
i\in I
.

We write

\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{S})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{S})

for the full inclusion of the sheaves into the category of presheaves. We call this the

category of sheaves
or the
sheaf topos
or the
Grothendieck topos
over
\mathcal{S}
.

The following is an important structure theorem for categories of sheaves:

Proposition B.19

(e.g.
[
22
]
)
.

Given a small category
\mathcal{S}
with the structure of a site
(definition
B.17
) then the full inclusion of the sheaves into
the presheaves (definition
B.18
) preserves filtered colimits
and has a left adjoint functor
L_{\mathcal{S}}
(‘sheafification‘”)
which preserves finite limits.

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\raise-5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.22226pt\raise-5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\raise 5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.35416pt\raise 10.89168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L_{\mathcal{S}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.09726pt\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bot}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 39.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 69.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{S})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Conversely, every full inclusion of this form into a category of presheaves on some small category
\mathcal{C}

is the inclusion of a sheaves for some site structure on
\mathcal{C}
.

Here is a list of some basic extra conditions on sites:

Definition B.20
.

A site
\mathcal{C}
(definition
B.17
) is called
sub-canonical

if every representable presheaf is a sheaf (definition
B.18
), i.e. if the Yoneda embedding
factors through the full inclusion of sheaves (proposition
B.19
)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.93697pt\raise-17.18748pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{y}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 86.7255pt\raise-7.18347pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.72229pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.72229pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 86.72229pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{C})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Definition B.21
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small site (definition
B.17
).

1.

A
point of the site

is a geometric morphism from the base topos to its sheaf topos (definition
B.18
), hence a pair of adjoint functors
(x^{\ast}\dashv x_{\ast})
(definition
B.4
)
of the form

\textstyle{\mathrm{Set}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{x^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})}

and such that
x^{\ast}
preserves finite colimits. In this case left adjoint
x^{\ast}
is called

forming the stalk at
x
, hence for
X\in\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})
any sheaf, then

x^{\ast}X\in Set
is called the
stalk
of
X
at
x
.

2.

The site
\mathcal{C}
is said to have
enough points
if there exists a set
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I}

of points, in the above sense, such that a morphism
f:X\to Y
in
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})

is an isomorphism precisely if all its stalks
x_{i}^{\ast}f:x_{i}^{\ast}X\to x_{i}^{\ast}Y
are bijections
of sets.

The following is a list of some basic properties of categories of sheaves that we need in the main text:

Proposition B.22
.

Let
S
be a small site (definition
B.17
), and let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism in the
category of sheaves
\mathrm{Sh}(S)
over it (definition
B.18
). Then in generality:

1.

f
is a monomorphism or isomorphism precisely it is so globally, hence
if for all object
U\in S
its component
f_{U}:X(U)\to Y(U)
is an
injection or bijection of sets, respectively;

2.

f
is an epimorphism precisely if it is so locally, hence if for each object
U\in S
there exists a
cover
\{U_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{i}}}{{\to}}U\}_{i}
such that for each element
y_{U}\in Y(U)

its restriction
y_{U_{i}}:=f(\phi_{i})(y_{U})
is in the image of
f_{U_{i}}:X(U_{i})\to Y(U_{i})

for all
i
.

But if
S
has
enough points

\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in the sense of definition
B.21
,
then
f
is an epi-/iso-/mono-morphisms precisely if it is so stalkwise, hence precisely if for
each
i\in I
then
x_{i}^{\ast}f:x_{i}^{\ast}X\to x_{i}^{\ast}Y
is a surjection/bijection/injection of sets,
respectively.

Proposition B.23

(universal colimits)
.

In a category of sheaves
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})
(definition

B.18
), colimits are compatible with fiber products : If
X:I\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})
is a diagram and

\varinjlim_{i}X_{i}\longrightarrow B
a morphism
out of its colimit, then for every morphism
f:A\longrightarrow B

the following square is Cartesian (is a pullback square)

Proposition B.24

(base change)
.

Let
\mathbf{H}=\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})
be a category of sheaves (definition
B.18
). Then for

f:X\longrightarrow Y

any morphism in
\mathbf{H}
, there is an adjoint triple of functors (definition
B.4
) between the slice categories
(definition
B.12
)

(f_{!}\dashv f^{\ast}\dashv f_{\ast})\;:\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 13.2941pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.2941pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 67.2941pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 33.6951pt\raise-16.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f_{!}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{H}_{/Y}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,,

where

1.

f_{!}
(left push-forward) is given by post-composition with
f
in
\mathbf{H}
;

2.

f^{\ast}
is given by pullback along
f
in
\mathbf{H}
.

Accordingly, by example
B.28
composition of left and right pushforward with pullback, respectively, yields
an adjoint pair of a monad
L_{f}
and a comonad
R_{f}
(definition
B.27
)

(L_{f}\dashv R_{f})\;:=\;\left((\eta_{f})^{\ast}\circ(\eta_{f})_{!}\,\dashv\,(\eta_{f})^{\ast}\circ(\eta_{f})_{\ast}\right)\;:\;\mathbf{H}_{/X}\longrightarrow\mathbf{H}_{/X}\,.

Proposition B.25
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small category equipped with a subcanonical coverage. Let
X\in\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})

be an object in the category of sheaves over
\mathcal{C}
. Write
\mathcal{C}/X
for the category
whose objects are morphisms
y(c)\to X
in
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})
, with
c\in\mathcal{C}

(and
y
denoting the Yoneda embedding) and whose morphisms are commuting triangles

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 13.92654pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.92654pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{y(c_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.92654pt\raise-26.3795pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.99597pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.46126pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 76.99597pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{y(c_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.99597pt\raise-26.37215pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.61111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 37.92654pt\raise-31.61111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

Say that a set of morphism in
\mathcal{C}/X
is a covering family if the underlying horizontal morphisms
in
\mathcal{C}
form a covering family. Then sheaves on
\mathcal{C}/X
are equivalently sheaves on
\mathcal{C}

sliced over
X
:

\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C}/X)\simeq\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})/X\,.

Proposition B.26
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a small site (definition
B.17
) and let
i:\mathcal{S}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}

be a fully faithful inclusion of the underlying category into a small category
\mathcal{C}
.
Then there exists a Grothendieck topology on
\mathcal{C}

and an equivalence of categories of sheaves (definition
B.18
) of the form

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.09726pt\raise 4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

If moreover the functor

\mathcal{C}\overset{y_{\mathcal{C}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{C})\overset{i^{\ast}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{S})\overset{L_{\mathcal{S}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{S})

is fully faithful (
y
denotes Yoneda embedding,
i^{\ast}
denotes restriction of presheaves,
proposition
B.24
,
and
L
denotes sheafification, definition
B.19
),
then this Grothendieck topology is subcanonical.

Proof.

Consider
6
6

6

We are grateful to Dave Carchedi for providing this argument.
the composition of pairs of adjoint functors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\raise-5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.79865pt\raise 0.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bot}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.38397pt\raise-10.81596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{\mathcal{S}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.22226pt\raise-5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\raise 5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.05554pt\raise 10.89168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L_{\mathcal{S}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 69.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 106.47235pt\raise-5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 127.38408pt\raise-10.81596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{\ast}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 127.764pt\raise 0.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bot}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 160.47235pt\raise-5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 106.47235pt\raise 5.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 127.38408pt\raise 10.67209pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i^{\ast}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 130.47235pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 160.47235pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{C})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,,

where on the left we have the sheafification adjunction over

\mathcal{S}
(proposition
B.19
),
while on the right we have the pullback / right Kan extension
adjunction along
i
(proposition
B.9
).
Here
L
preserves finite limits (by proposition

B.19
) and
i^{\ast}
preserves all
limits (since it has a further left adjoint given by left Kan
extension). Hence the composite adjunction is a geometric embedding

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{S})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{L_{\mathcal{C}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{C})}

of the topos
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{S})

into the presheaf topos
\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{C})
. By proposition
B.19
every such
corresponds to the sheafification adjunction for some Grothendieck topology on

\mathcal{C}
, identifying the former with the category of sheaves
over the latter.

In particular

L_{\mathcal{S}}\circ i^{\ast}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}\simeq L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}\,,

and hence if

L_{\mathcal{S}}\circ i^{\ast}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}
is fully faithful
then so is
L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}
and hence also

i_{\mathcal{C}}\circ L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}
.
This means that for
C\in\mathcal{C}
, then the sheafification of
y(\mathcal{C})

is the presheaf given on
D\in\mathcal{C}
by

\displaystyle(L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}(C))(D)

\displaystyle\simeq(i_{\mathcal{C}}\circ L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}(C))(D)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{C})}(y_{\mathcal{C}}(D),i_{\mathcal{C}}\circ L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}(C))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})}(L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}(D),L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}(C))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{C})}(i_{\mathcal{C}}\circ L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}(D),i_{\mathcal{C}}\circ L_{\mathcal{C}}\circ y_{\mathcal{C}}(C))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(C,D)

\displaystyle\simeq y_{\mathcal{C}}(D)\,.

This says that
y_{\mathcal{C}}(C)
coincides with its sheafification, hence that the Grothendieck
topology on
\mathcal{C}
is subcanonical.
∎

B.3
Monads

We collect some basic facts on (co-)monads and (co-)monadic descent, see for instance
[
3
, vol 2, section 4]
.

Definition B.27
.

For
\mathcal{C}
a category, then a
monad
on
\mathcal{C}

is an endofunctor

T\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}

equipped with natural transformations

•

(product)
\nabla:T\circ T\longrightarrow T
;

•

(unit)
\eta:\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow T

such that these satisfy the following associativity and unitality
properties:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern Dually, a
comonad
on
\mathcal{C}
is an endofunctor
J\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
equipped with natural transformations

•

(coproduct)
\Delta:J\longrightarrow J\circ J

•

(counit)
\epsilon:J\longrightarrow\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}

such that these satisfy the following coassociativity and counitality
properties:

Example B.28
.

Given a pair of adjoint functors
(L\dashv R)
(definition

B.4
) then

1.

R\circ L
becomes a monad (definition
B.27
)
by taking the monad unit to be the adjunction unit, and taking the monad product to be
the image of the adjunction counit on
L
under
R
:

\textstyle{(R\circ L)\circ(R\circ L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\epsilon_{L}}
\textstyle{R\circ L}

2.

L\circ R
becomes a comonad (definition
B.27
)
by taking the comonad counit to be the adjunction counit, and taking the comonad coproduct to be
the image of the adjunction unit on
R
under
L
:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.96004pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.96004pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L\circ R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.32785pt\raise 6.07222pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.71112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L\eta_{R}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.96004pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.96004pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(L\circ R)\circ(L\circ R)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Definition B.29
.

Given a comonad
(J,\epsilon,\Delta)
on
\mathcal{C}
, definition
B.27
,
then a
coalgebra
over the comonad is an object
E\in\mathcal{C}
equipped with a morphism

\rho\colon E\longrightarrow JE

such that

1.

(coaction property) the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{JE\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J\rho}
\textstyle{JE\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{E}}
\textstyle{JJE}

2.

(counitality) the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{JE\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{id}\,JE}
\textstyle{JJE\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{JE}}

A homomorphism of coalgebras
f:(E_{1},\rho_{1})\longrightarrow(E_{2},\rho_{2})
is a morphism
f:E_{1}\longrightarrow E_{2}
in

\mathcal{C}
which respects these coaction morphisms in that the
following diagram commutes:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.05182pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.22221pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.13365pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.72917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.05182pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.72917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.95139pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.95139pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-12.4757pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{JE_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.5913pt\raise-37.44441pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Jf}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.4757pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.4757pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{JE_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\,.

The resulting category of coalgebras is denoted
\mathrm{EM}(J)

(for “Eilenberg-Moore category”).

Proposition B.30

(Beck equalizer)
.

For
J\colon\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

any comonad (definition
B.27
) and

\rho:E\longrightarrow JE
a coalgebra over
J
(definition
B.29
)
then the diagram

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{JE\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{E}}
\scriptstyle{J\rho}
\textstyle{JJE}

is an equalizer diagram, in fact it is an absolute equalizer
(meaning that it is preserved by every functor
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
).
In particular therefore
\rho
is a monomorphism.

Proposition B.31
.

For
(L\dashv R):\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise 4.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.9722pt\raise 9.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.68523pt\raise-9.39166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise-4.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{D}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
an adjunction, definition
B.4
, then the endofunctor

T:=L\circ R\;:\;\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

becomes a comonad on
\mathcal{C}
(definition
B.27
) with
counit the adjunction counit
L\circ R\to\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}
(definition
B.4
), and with coproduct
induced from the unit of the adjunction by

\Delta_{T}:=L(\eta_{R(-)})\,.

Dually,
R\circ L
is canonically equipped with the structure of a monad.

Example B.32
.

Given an adjoint triple
(L\ \dashv C\dashv R)
of functors (definition
B.4
)
then the monad
T:=C\circ L
and the comonad
J:=C\circ R

(induced via proposition
B.31
) themselves form an adjoint pair:

(T\dashv J):\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\,.

Proposition B.33
.

In the situation of example
B.32
then
the double
(T\dashv J)
-adjunct
\tilde{\tilde{f}}
(definition

B.4
) of a morphism
f:X\to JJY
of the form

\textstyle{JY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{Y}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{JJY}

is given by the
(T\dashv J)
-adjunct
\tilde{g}
of

via

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern In fact both these kinds of morphisms are in natural bijection with
those of the form

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 29.68639pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.68639pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{TX\simeq CLX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.0734pt\raise 5.83888pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{C\overline{g}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.68639pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.68639pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 83.68639pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{CRX\simeq JX}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,,

where
\overline{g}
denotes the
(C\dashv R)
-adjunct of
g
.

Proof.

By definition of the monad unit (definition
B.31
),

f
is of the form

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.4585pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 61.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{CRY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.09593pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{C(\eta_{RY})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.09514pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 115.09514pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 145.09514pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{CRCRY}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

By composition of adjunctions, the
(CL\dashv CR)
-adjunct of this morphism is the

(L\dashv C)
-adjunct of its
(C\dashv R)
-adjunct.
Since the morphism on the right is in the image of
C
, the naturality of the adjunction isomorphism
(definition
B.4
) implies that
f
is in natural bijection to

\textstyle{LX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{g}}
\textstyle{RY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{RY}}
\textstyle{RCRY\,.}

Now by the formula for adjuncts from proposition
B.8
, the further

(C\dashv R)
-adjunct of this morphism is

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.86874pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.86874pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{CLX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.25575pt\raise 5.83888pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{C\overline{g}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.86874pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.86874pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 68.86874pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{CRY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.67183pt\raise 6.07222pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.71112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{C\eta_{RY}}$}}}\kern Here the composite on the right is the identity morphism, as shown, by proposition
B.5
.
This shows that the single
(T\dashv J)
-adjunct of
f
is
C\overline{g}
. In complete formal duality
one finds that also the single
(T\dashv J)
-adjunct (in the other direction) of
TTX\overset{\nabla_{X}}{\longrightarrow}TX\overset{\tilde{g}}{\longrightarrow}Y

is
C\overline{g}
. Hence the statement follows.
∎

Definition B.34
.

The full subcategory of
\mathrm{EM}(J)
on the cofree coalgebras,
i.e., on the
objects in the image of
\mathrm{F}
, proposition
B.35
,
is denoted
\mathrm{Kl}(J)
(from “Kleisli category”, or more
appropriately “co-Kleisli category” given that
J
is a comonad).

Proposition B.35
.

The category of coalgebras over a comonad on a category
\mathcal{C}
,
definition
B.29
, is related to
\mathcal{C}
by a pair
of adjoint functors, definition
B.4
, of the form

(\mathrm{U}\dashv\mathrm{F}):\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 46.2101pt\raise-8.39166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{F}}$}}}\kern where the left adjoint
U
(“underlying”) forgets the coalgebra
structure,
U:(E,\rho)\mapsto E
, while the right adjoint
F

(“cofree”) sends an object
c\in\mathcal{C}
to to the object
Jc
with coaction given by the coproduct
\Delta_{J}
. The comonad
induced from this adjunction via
proposition
B.31
coincides with
J
:

J\simeq\mathrm{U}\circ\mathrm{F}\,.

Remark B.36
.

In the situation of proposition
B.35
, given
objects
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathcal{C}
, then by adjunction we have a
bijection of morphisms of the form

Fc_{1}

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}

Fc_{2}

Jc_{1}\simeq{}

UFc_{1}

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{f}}}{{\longrightarrow}}

c_{2}

Hence morphisms
f
in the co-Kleisli category
\mathrm{Kl}(J)
,
definition
B.34
, are equivalently morphisms in

\mathcal{C}
of the form
\tilde{f}:Jc_{1}\longrightarrow c_{2}
.
According to proposition
B.8
, the
adjunct morphisms are related by
\tilde{f}=\epsilon_{c_{2}}\circ f
,
where we have identified
f\simeq Uf
, and
f=J(\tilde{f})\circ\Delta_{c_{1}}
, where we have identified
J(\tilde{f})\simeq F(\tilde{f})
.

Under identification between the adjunct morphisms
f
and

\tilde{f}
, the composition of morphisms
g\circ f
in

\mathrm{Kl}(J)
is given by the “co-Kleisli composite”

\widetilde{g\circ f}:Jc_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta_{c_{1}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}JJc_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle J(\tilde{f})}}{{\longrightarrow}}Jc_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{g}}}{{\longrightarrow}}c_{3}\,.

The fact that a morphism between free coalgebras
f\colon Fc_{1}\to Fc_{2}
must be of the form
f=J(\tilde{f})\circ\Delta_{c_{1}}
, as
observed above, follows from the general
proposition
B.35
. But there is also an
elementary way to see it. Consider the following diagram:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.47147pt\raise 31.86111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.36113pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}=\epsilon_{c_{2}}\circ f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 153.00037pt\raise 4.2253pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise which commutes because
f
is a morphism of free coalgebras and
because of the counit-coproduct identities of a comonad. From the
commutativity, as was desired, it follows that
f=J(\tilde{f})\circ\Delta_{c_{1}}
, with
\tilde{f}=\epsilon_{c_{2}}\circ f
.

Definition B.37
.

A functor
F:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
is called
conservative

if it reflects equivalences, hence if for a morphism
f
in
\mathcal{D}
we
have that if
F(f)
is an equivalence then already
f
was an equivalence.

Theorem B.38

(Beck monadicity theorem, e.g.
[
3
, vol. 4 sect. 2]
)
.

Sufficient conditions for an adjunction
(L\dashv R)
, definition
B.4
,
to be equivalent to a comonadic adjunction
(\mathrm{U}\dashv\mathrm{F})
as in proposition
B.35
is that

1.

L
is conservative, definition
B.37
;

2.

L
preserves certain limits called
equalizers of
L
-split pairs
.

Hence it is useful to record some facts about conservative functors:

Proposition B.39

(e.g.
[
13
, lemma 1.3.2]
)
.

For
\mathbf{H}
a category of sheaves and
f:X\longrightarrow Y
an epimorphism
in
\mathbf{H}
, then the pullback functor
f^{\ast}:\mathbf{H}_{/Y}\longrightarrow\mathbf{H}_{/X}

(proposition
B.24
)
is conservative, definition
B.37
.

Proposition B.40
.

A conservative functor reflects all the limits and colimits which it preserves.

Proposition B.41

(comonadic descent, e.g.
[
12
, 2.4]
)
.

Given an epimorphism
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y}
in a
category of sheaves
\mathbf{H}
, with induced base change comonad

J:=f^{\ast}f_{\ast}:\mathbf{H}_{/X}\to\mathbf{H}_{/X}

(via proposition
B.24
and proposition
B.31
),
then there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{H}_{/Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{EM}(J)

between the slice category
\mathbf{H}_{/Y}
(definition
B.12
)
and the Eilenberg-Moore category of
J
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{H}_{/X}
, definition
B.29
.

Moreover, under this identification the comonadic adjunction
(U_{J}\dashv F_{J})
from proposition
B.35

coincides with the base change adjunction
(f^{\ast}\dashv f_{\ast})
of proposition
B.24
:

(U_{J}\dashv F_{J})\simeq(f^{\ast}\dashv f_{\ast})\,.

Proof.

Since
f
is assumed to be epi,
proposition
B.39
says that
f^{\ast}

is conservative. Moreover, since
f^{\ast}
is right adjoint to
f_{!}
by
proposition
B.24
, it preserves all small
limits (proposition
B.7
). Therefore
the conditions in the monadicity theorem
B.38

are satisfied:
